# Second Part (II)

**Author**: Anonymous (Chronicle of Seert)  
**Series**: PO Tome 13  
**Columns**: pp. 436–637  

[p. 436] {#po.13.p436}

### XLI. - History of Tiberius, Emperor of the Greeks
حبر طيباربوس ملك الروم ملك هذا الرجل فى سنة خمس وثمائين وثمان مائة اسكندرة فاحسن الى المساكين وصحح القول بالجوهرين فى المسيح وطرد البراطقة. وبطل فى ابامه الصلج مع الفرس لسبب خروج الارمن عن طاعة الفرس واعتضادهم بالروم. فرزقه الله الظفر بهم وغيرهم من الاعدا. ثم قصد الفرس وراس العين ونواحى نصيبين واخربوا وسبوا. فانفذ اليهم مورثا رئيس جيشه فسبى منهم سبعين- الفا واسكنهم حزيرة قبرس ثم عاد الفرس دفعة ثانية وصاروا .الى نواحي قليقيا فيزمهم عنها. ولمعا تبين لطيباربوس فضل مورسًا وشجاعته زوجه بابنته واوصى من بعد أن يملك لانه لم يكن له ابن .يرث الملك بعد ومات ومدة ملكه تبع سنين منها مع يوسطينا خمس سنين وعدا اربع سنين.
This man reigned in the year 885 of Alexander; he favored the poor, defended the belief in the duality of natures in Christ, and drove out the heretics. In his time, peace was broken with the Persians because of the Armenians, who revolted against them and implored the protection of the Greeks. God made the emperor triumph over the Persians and other enemies. The Persians then invaded Resh'aina (Ra's el-'Ain) and the surroundings of Nisibis. They devastated the whole country and led the inhabitants into captivity. Tiberius (Tibâryoüs) sent against them Maurice (Môriqa), the chief of his armies; he took seventy thousand prisoners and sent them to the island of Cyprus (Qouprous). He also repelled the Persians, who had resumed the offensive and invaded the regions of Cilicia (Qâliqyya). Tiberius, having realized the merit and bravery of Maurice, gave him his daughter in marriage, and, having no son to replace him on the throne, he designated him as his successor. He died after a reign of nine years, five of which were with Justin (Youstinä) and four years after him.


[p. 438] {#po.13.p438}

### XLII. - History of Isho'yahb of Arzun, the thirtieth catholicos
خبز ايشوعيب الجاثليق الارزني وهو الثائون هذا الرجل من اهل باعربايا وكان عالما فاضلا تام القامة حسن الصورة وتعام بين يدي ابرهيم المفس بنصيبين. ثم صار مفسرا بعد واختير لاسقفة ارزن واسيم الهاء وكان هرمز الملك يعرفه وبميل اليه لانه كان يكاتبه باخاد حيوش الروم وحركتها.
This man was a native of Beith 'Arbaye; he was learned and virtuous; he had a perfect stature and a handsome face; he had completed his studies at Nisibis under Abraham the Interpreter, whom he had subsequently succeeded in the chair of interpretation, and then he was chosen and consecrated bishop of Arzun. King Hormizd knew him and loved him, for it was he who informed him of the movements of the Greek armies.
ولما استناح! حزقيال الجاثليق اجتمع الناس لوقوع الاختبار على من يقد له الجثلفة فاختار بعضهم ايوب المفسس الذي كان بالمدائن وهو قرابة مار نرسى واختار اخرون ابشوعيب اسنقف ارزن وانتبى الام الى الملك فامر ان ينصب فى الجثلقة اسقف ارزن وسال عن الرسم فى ذلك. فعرف انه يحتاج الى جمع الاساقفة والمطارنة من البلدان. فاحضرهم فلما وردوا عقدوا له الفطركة ثم دخل وهو معهم الى التملك ودعوا له فأكريهم واعادهم الى
After the death of Ezekiel the catholicos, some of those who had gathered to choose a catholicos elected Job (Ayyoûb) the interpreter of Seleucia (Al-Madain) and the relative of Mar Narsai; the others chose Isho'yahb, bishop of Arzun. The king, to whom the situation had just been reported, ordered that the bishop of Arzun be consecrated catholicos; and having inquired about how he should be consecrated, he was told that it was necessary to summon the bishops and metropolitans. He therefore had them called and imposed upon them the duty to consecrate Isho'yahb patriarch. The latter, accompanied by the bishops, presented himself before the king, to whom they rendered


[p. 439] {#po.13.p439}
كراسيهم. وكتب الى عماله بان يرجعوا الى راي الاساقفة فى الاحكام وفى مائر الامور ولا يعملوا شيئنً الا بمشورتهم. واغاظ ذلك المجوس لانه اكرم النصارى “أكثر من كل ملك من ملوك الفرس. وفى السنة الرابعة من «ثاسته عمل اثنين وعشرين قانونً ليعغوب اسقف ديرين الجزيرة ضمّنها ما يحتاج اليه فى تدبير رعيته وفسس الرازين! متتصرً. وكانت نصيبين وعانة الحدّ بينة مملكتي الروم والفرس. ولما جرى على هربز من ابنه كسرى ابرويز من القبض وسمل العين والتغلب على الملك بمعاونة الجند وتفور بهرام صاحب الجيش عليه حتى احتاج بعد تغلبه وحصوله فى الملك الى البرب من بيد برام
thanks, and who sent them back to their sees filled with honor. He even wrote to the governors to act according to the advice of the bishops in matters of law and other affairs, and to do nothing except according to their counsel. The Magi were irritated that, more than all the Persian kings, he honored the Christians. In the fourth year of his pontificate, he composed for Jacob (Ia'qoûb), bishop of the island of Dirin, twenty-two canons, which contained what he needed for the administration of his diocese. He explained in summary the holy mysteries. Nisibis and 'Ana were two cities bordering the Greeks and the Persians. Khosrow Parwez attacked his father Hormizd, put out his eyes, and, thanks to the soldiers, seized the empire. But after his victory and the proclamation of his accession, he was forced to flee before Bahram, the commander of the armies, and to have recourse to the Greek emperor, who gave him troops and made him master of the empire, as we shall speak of in


[p. 440] {#po.13.p440}
صاحب الجيش نحو ملك الروم وعوده بالجيوش منهم والظفر بالمملكة كما سنذكر جملة مأ جرت الحال عليه عقدت له القاب: فى رجوعه ظافراً عقد له ابشوعيب الجائليق ثلث قاب ووقف للسلام عليه. فاظبن التغير والغضب على الجائليق ولم يلتفت اليه وقد كان اتخذ له طرفا من المسك والنبر والكافور والزعفران والعود البندى والمجامس بايدى الاساققه. فعدل عن الدخول تحت القبة الاولى. فاسرع الجائليق الى القية الثانية ويده اتن واترجّة ونجمرة فعدل عنبا. ففلق الجائليق لذلك وهمبمت ححيوش موريقى ملك الروم لما راوا من استخفافه به فلما عرف ذلك منهم قال كسرى للجائليق قد استعمات ثلاث خصال لا بد من مكافاتك عليها الاولى انك لم تخرج معى الى بلاد الروم ولا انفذت من الاساقفة احدً فى صحبتى. وكان الملك موريقى» يزيد فى اكرامى ولانك لم تلحقنى ولم تج الى لما غرفت حصولى فى مملكة الروم عند مورقى وقبوله اياى. والثانية انك لم تصلح لى قية حسب ما يجب. ولثالثة دعاوك لبهرام الخارجى. ثم وافى القبة ألثلثة ودخلبا واسك عنان
detail. As they were setting up tents for his triumphal return, the catholicos Isho'yahb also set up three tents for him, before which he stood, ready to greet him. But the king appeared angry with the catholicos and did not want to look at him. On his order, the bishops held in their hands sought-after musk, exquisite amber, camphor, saffron, Indian aloe wood, and censers; the king did not enter under the first tent. The catholicos, who held in his hand myrtle, a citron, and a censer, hastened to go to the second tent. The king did not want to enter it. The catholicos was in perplexity; the soldiers of Maurice began to murmur at the sight of this lack of regard. The king, who noticed it, then said to the catholicos: "You have done three bad deeds, for which you must be punished. First, you did not come with me to Greek land; you did not even send a bishop to accompany me. Emperor Maurice would have honored me much more. You did not even come to join me in the land of the Greeks, where you knew well that I had arrived and been received by Maurice. Secondly, you did not set up a tent for me as you should have. Finally, you prayed for the usurper Bahram." Then the king headed toward the third tent and entered it. They held his mount by the bridle. When he was


[p. 441] {#po.13.p441}
دابنه ووقف تحتبا والتفت الى الجاثليق وقال وهو غضان اتتوهم ان حيلتك هذ توازى عنك العشوبة على ذنونك. او تظن انه خفى علتي بسط يديك ودعاؤك لبهرام خالع طاعتى. فقال له اما دعاى فما كان الا للملك وان يعيذ الناس من عادية ذلك العبد الشرير. فقال له قد توسطت قبتك وقبات تحيتك واخرتك الى هذا المدة التى احدها وهى ثلثة ايام لتجيب عن سوالى وهو الثلث مسائل التى اسالك عنها. ثم م يده فتناول الاترجة. قدعا له الجاثليق واكثر وانصرف. ولما مضت ثلثة ايام ساله عن حجته فى الثلث خصال التى غضب لاحلبا. فقال له اما تاخرى عن الخروج فى صحبتك فانه كان بالليل ولم يتقدم علمى به ولان من يخدمنى يمنعه الصوم عن السفر معى ولانى تخوفت ان يجرى ما لا اوش مشاهدته فى دينى من سفك الدماء واما تعودى عن اللحاق لما عرفت حصوله فى بلد الروم فلو زلت عن رعيتى وعرف المخلف الشاق للعصا خبرى لم يدع منهم صغيرا ولا كبيرا الا سفك دمه. والذى يجب على خلاص نفوسهم ببذل نفسى لا ان اعرضهم لقتل وافوز بالحياة دونهم. واما
under the tent, he addressed the catholicos and said to him with anger: "Do you think that you will be able to escape by the ruse you have just employed from the punishment of which your crimes have rendered you worthy? Or do you believe that I was unaware that you extended your hands to pray for Bahram, who had revolted against me? - I only prayed, he replied, for the king. I wished that men might escape the tyranny of this wicked slave. - I have entered, he said to him, under your tent and I have accepted your compliments; and, to answer the three questions that I have asked you, I give you three days' delay." Then he extended his hand, took the citron from the hands of the catholicos, who prayed much for him, and he continued his route. At the end of three days, he asked him what his defense was regarding the three crimes that had angered him. "What prevented me, he replied, from accompanying you is that you left during the night without me noticing; besides, the fast would have prevented my retinue from accompanying me on this journey; and I feared seeing the shedding of the blood of my coreligionists. It is for the same cause that I was prevented later from joining the king in Greek land, because if I had left my flock, the rebellious adversary would have known it and would have shed the blood of the little ones as well as that of the great. Now my duty is to save it by giving my life, and not to save myself while it is threatened with perishing. Finally, God, who knows intentions and who probes hearts, is my witness that I only prayed for the king;


[p. 442] {#po.13.p442}
دعاى فما كان الا للملك اذ كنت دعوت بحراسة الملك وحياطته وثيات ملكه. والله يعام الاعتقاد وحرف ما فى الضمير. وكيف بجوز ان ادعو لمن اعلم انه عاص لمولاه بل كنت اضمس ان دعاى للذى يستحق ان يستى ملكا. فقال له قد قات اعتذارك وزال ما خامر قلبى من امرك.
I prayed for the preservation and safety of the king and for the strengthening of his power. How could I have allowed myself to pray for the one whom I knew to be a rebel against his master? The prayer that I made, I offered in my heart for the one who was worthy to be called king. - I have accepted, the king replied, your excuses, and I have rejected the designs that I had imagined against you."
وقد كان الروم غير راضين عنه لاجل ما كان يكاتنب هرمن الملك باخارهم ايام اسقفته على ارزن. ثم ان ايشوعيب اعثل واستناح فى السنة الخامسة من ملك كسرى أبرونز ودفنته هند ابنة النعمان بن المنذر ملك العرب فى البيعة التى ابتنتها بالحيرة وعرف الان بدير هند فى وسط اليم! وعلى قبره دكة الصليب وكانت مده اربع عشرة سنة وقيل خمس عشرة.
The Greeks were dissatisfied with him (the patriarch) because, when he was bishop of Arzun, he informed King Hormizd of their movements. Isho'yahb fell ill and died in the fifth year of Khosrow Parwez. Hind, daughter of En-Nu'man, son of el-Mundhir, king of the Arabs, buried him in the church that she had built at Hira, in the middle of the choir, where they raised a bench in the shape of a cross over his tomb. This church is known today by the name of the Convent of Hind. His pontificate lasted fourteen years according to some, fifteen years according to others.


[p. 443] {#po.13.p443}
ذكر جمل ما جرى من كسرى ابروز على ابيه هريز وسبب ذلك على ما ثبت فى اخار الملوك
XLIII. - Summary of what happened to Hormizd by his son Khosrow and the cause of these events according to the royal annals
كان لبرمز صاحب حجيش اسمه برام جودين انفذه لقتال الترك وظفر بهم وغنم غنيمة عظيمة وحمل الى هريز ما حصل من الغنائم وحسن موقعه عند فحيده اصحاب الملك وضريوا عليه وصغروا عظيم ما انفذ فى جنب ما حصله لنفسه من بلاد الترك. فكافلا على جميل بلاثه بان انفذ اليه قميصا احمز ومغزلا وفلكة وقال له مثلك يجب ان يكون لباسه هذا اللباس. فنا الجيش وامتعضشوا له وخلموا طاعة الملك هرين. وورد عليه الأمر بإن يصير الى حضرته. فانكفا راجعاً على العصيان. فلما بلغ الرى ضرب دراهم عليها اسم ولد كسرى ابرويز بن هرمن وصورته وانفذها سر الى المداين وظهرت فى ايدى العوام. ولغ الخر هرمز وعمل على القبض على ابنه. وعرف الابن ذلك فهرب نحو اذربيجان.
Hormizd had sent the commander of his armies, Bahram Chobin (Djôbîn), to fight the Turks; he had triumphed over them and had sent rich booty to Hormizd, who greatly honored the victor. But the jealous courtiers slandered him to him, saying that the booty sent was very mediocre compared to that which he had taken for himself. Now the king, in reward for so many services, sent him a red shirt and a spindle equipped with the ball that terminates it: "A man like you," he had him told, "should have only such attire." This angered the army and led it to revolt against King Hormizd. Bahram, having received the order from the king to appear before him, went into revolt and left for Rayy, where he had coins struck, on which he engraved the name and image of Khosrow Parwez, son of Hormizd, and sent them furtively to Seleucia (Al-Madain), where, having seen them pass through the hands of the people, some informed the king, who resolved to have his son arrested. The latter, having learned of this, fled to Azerbaijan.


[p. 444] {#po.13.p444}
وكانت اخلاق هربز قد حفت على الجيوش وضيق علييم واخذ اموالم وقتل اخوته وقبض على الروسآ” واخذ ضياعهم وثى بالاهواز ومهرجا حبوساً وحس فيا روساء اهل مملكته وكان بطعمهم خبرا فيه حصا ويسقيهم الما المر. فقبضوا عليه وكاتبوا كسرى ابرويز بالمسين. فسار اليهم وصار الملك عليهم ولم يامن الجيش حيلة تتم لبرمز فسملوا عينيه. وعرف يرام شوبين ذلك فادر فى حيشه مظبراً الامتعاضش مما جزى على الملك. فقابله أبرديز. وانبزم ابرويز من بين .يديه وقصد موريقى ملك الروم مستنصراً به على الغاصسب لمملكته. فانفذ معه الجيش بعد مصاهرته له بابنته مريم نت موديقى وورد المداين فانهزم بهرام وظفروا به. واستقامت له المملكة. وكانت صورة ايشوعيب فى يوم دخوله مظفرا معه تحت القباب الثى نصبها له ما قد شرحتلا وعاد الى محته له وتقديمة ابلا. وكذلك كانت اخلاقه مع سبريشوع الجاتليق بعده وافضل.
Hormizd had treated the army with rigor and severity; he had confiscated its goods, killed his own brothers, arrested the leaders, whose villages he had confiscated, and built dungeons in Khuzistan and Maherdja, where he had thrown the notables of his kingdom, to whom he gave bread mixed with gravel to eat, and brackish water to drink. They arrested him and had Khosrow Parwez sought out and named him king. The army, fearing the ruses of Hormizd, put out his eyes. Bahram Chobin, having known this, gathered the troops and marched (toward Seleucia), pretending to be irritated by what had happened to the king. Parwez, unable to hold out against him, fled and went to Maurice (Môriqi), emperor of the Greeks, to solicit his help against the usurper of his throne. Maurice gave him his daughter Mary in marriage and gave him troops; he came to Seleucia, put Bahram to flight, and triumphed; and thus he became the sole master of the empire. We have already told how he treated Isho'yahb the day he entered victorious under the tents that the latter had set up for him. From then on, he loved him as before and honored him. He had the same regards and even better ones for the catholicos Sabrisho'.


[p. 445] {#po.13.p445}

### FACTS THAT TOOK PLACE DURING THIS PERIOD
الامور التى جرت فى هذا المدة من ذلك خبى دبّن اليا الدى بتى العمر! المعروف بدير سعيد بالموصل هذا القتيس اليا من الحيرة. وتطّم بيا. وقصد مار ابراهم وقبل منه الرهبنة وخدم مع الاقنوين وكان على باب يت الجاوك مجونا مطروحا. فضرب ذا الطوبى على فكه ضري شديداً فحل له القديس فكه الآخر. فلما راى الشيطان تواضعه فارق ذلك الرجل ورا من غلنه. وظبرت هذه الاعجونة للاخوة فخرج من. العسن وقصد ارض ينوى سكن فى حبل هناك بالقرب من دحلة. وكان بقرب الموضع احمة فها سباع فهرب حميها بصلاته. ثم اجتمع الرهبان اليه ونى فى الموضع عمر قريب من الموضع؟ فى ايام هرمز بن كسرى انوشروان. ورسم فيه قوانين مار ابراهم. وسث اليه ايشوعيب اسقف بلد الدَى تقد الجثلقة غلاماً مسترخى اليدين" فابرل.
XLIV. - History of Rabban Elijah who founded in Mosul the monastery known by the name of Dair Sa'id. This saint Elijah, after having completed his studies at Hira, his native city, went to Mar Abraham, from whom he received the monastic habit. He served the cenobites. A madman, who was standing at the door of the community, gave him a violent slap on the cheek; the saint presented the other cheek. The demon, having seen his humility, withdrew from the man, who was immediately healed. This miracle having been known to the brothers, Elijah abandoned the convent and went to the land of Nineveh, where he retired to a mountain near the Tigris. There was a forest there full of ferocious beasts. The saint drove them out by his prayers: he built in this place, which is near Mosul, a monastery for the monks who had gathered around him, and established there the rule of Mar Abraham. This convent was founded in the time of Hormizd, son of Khosrow Anushirwan. He healed a child paralyzed in the hands, whom Isho'yahb, bishop of Balad, who was later Catholicos, sent to him.


[p. 446] {#po.13.p446}
وكات لبذا القديس حدة العرييّة. ولما ضعف جعل امرا الى حنايشوع الذى نصب عمرا ناحية داراباد. ثم مات قدس الله روحه ودفن فى عبر الذى ناا اول صلواته مناه
This saint had the violent character of the Arabs. When he saw himself weakened, he entrusted the affairs to Hnanisho', who founded a convent in the region of Darabad. After his death, they buried him in the monastery that he had built. May God sanctify his soul and may his prayers be with us.

### XLV. - History of Rabban Bar 'Edta
خبر رين ! برعاتا هذا القدين من اهل البلد المعروف بنينوى. وكانت اتنه عاقرا. فلازمت الصوم والصّلاة والبيعة تدعو الله وتساله ان يرذقها ولد فسمع الله دعاها كما سمع من حنًا ورزتها انا قسمته هذا الاسم ومعناد ” ابن البيعة. ونذرته لله كما نذرت حنًّا سمئيل. ولما كبر قصد اسكول نصييين وصادف هناك مار ابراهم فتتلمذ له ولازمه مثل يوناثان لداود ومهنا فى العلم والتشته بالزى الروحانى وتفتزد فى مغارته. ولما استناح مار ابراهم وتفترق اولاده خرج
This saint was a native of the region known by the name of Nineveh. His mother was sterile; she applied herself to fasting and prayer, frequenting the church and begging God to grant her a child. God heard her prayer, as he had listened to Hannah, and gave her a son, whom she called Bar 'Edta, a name which means: son of the Church. She vowed him to God as Hannah had vowed Samuel. When he had grown up, he went to the School of Nisibis, where he met Mar Abraham, of whom he became the disciple. He attached himself to him as Jonathan had attached himself to David and, after having successfully followed his lessons and received from his hands the costume of the spirituals, he retired into a cave to live there in solitude. After the death of Mar Abraham and the dispersion of his children,


[p. 447] {#po.13.p447}
رين برعانا من العمر وهو بكر اولاد ابراهم. واجتمع اليه جماعة وقصد حبل المرج وتلمذ خلقا من الاكراد الذين فيه ونى هناك عمراً. وجعل فيه جماعة من الرهبان. ثم مات فى يوم احد الحديث! ودفن فى عمرة. وسل ذكرانه! فى كل سنة فى مثل هذا اليوم. وقد خرج جماعة من هذا العمر قديسون منهم شمعون صاحب عمر السن. ودبّن يوزادق ورين هرمز ونوحنً الفارسى وغيرهم صلواتهم تعضدنا دائما امين ١ خبر رين شمعون
of whom Rabban Bar 'Edta was the first-born, the latter, accompanied by several monks, went to the mountain of Marga, where he converted many Kurds and built a monastery, where he established a small community of monks. He died on the Sunday of Quasimodo, the day on which his commemoration is held each year, and he was buried in his convent. Several saintly personages came out of this convent; among others, Simeon (Sem'oun), founder of the convent of Senna, Rabban Yozadaq, Rabban Hormizd, and John (Yohanna) the Persian. May their prayers always strengthen us. Amen.

### XLVI. - History of Rabban Simeon
هذا الرجل من اهل كشكر. ولما خرج من بلده طالً لله تعالى وجد فى طرقه نر فرسم عليه صليباً وعبرا كما يمشى الانسان على الارض وذلك قبل رهينته. وقصد ربّن برعاتا واقام هناك فى مغارة يتقترت بثمرة شجرةٌ يقال ا دنوطاة زمانً ثم انصرف الى الاردن فاقام على شاطيه يعبد الله وباكل من بقل يزرعه. فوقع فى الموضع حراد. وقصد المبقلة التى
This man was a native of Kashkar. He left his country to seek God the Most High. Having encountered a river on his route, he made the sign of the cross over the water and crossed it, as if he had walked on dry land; this took place before he was a monk. He went to find Rabban Bar 'Edta and retired not far from him into a cave, where he fed for a long time on the fruit of a tree called dabouta. He then reached the banks of the Jordan,


[p. 448] {#po.13.p448}
بترت منها القديس. فصاح عليه بصوت عال فوقف حوليا ولم 'يعرض ليا اياماً. ثم اختار هذا القديس ان يصبر على المحنة مع سكان تلك الارض. فاطق للجراد اكل المبقلة. ومسكث يتقرت ما بقى حتى تقد. وقى بلا شىء ياكله. وعثلم عليه الجوع فصلّى وقال. ا رب من اجل محبّتك قتل الانياء والسليحون!. وقطع الشهداء. من اجلبا خرجت من العالم وسبيبا اموت.
and remained there adoring God; he fed on the vegetables that he sowed himself. Once, the locusts that had ravaged the region headed toward the vegetable garden from which the saint drew his subsistence; the latter cried out before them with a loud voice; and immediately they stopped around the garden and spared it. But the saint, having preferred afterward to undergo the trial with the inhabitants of the region, allowed the locusts to ravage the vegetable garden. He lived for some time longer on what remained of the vegetables, until there were none left. Having then nothing to eat, he felt hunger violently. He prayed, saying: "Lord! It is for love of you that the prophets and the apostles were put to death and that the martyrs have
وقام فصار الى مغارته رجل اعطانا خيز وغاب عنه ولم يرظ. فعلم انه ملك الربٍ. ونزل اليه ذلك الخيز كما نزل خيز الارملة فى ايام اليا النبتى واكل منه زمانا. ولما أختار الانتقال من موضعه لم يجد منه شيا. واختطف مثل حبقوق النبى الى ارض الفرس. وسكن فى مغارة على حبل السنّ. ونى
been torn to pieces; it is also for love of you that I have abandoned the world and that I am going to suffer death." As soon as he rose (from his prayer), he saw a man enter his cave; he gave him bread and disappeared. He knew that it was the angel of the Lord. The bread descended for him as the bread of the widow had already descended in the time of the prophet Elijah. He ate of it for a long time; but as soon as he wanted to change his retreat, he found no trace of it. Like Habakkuk, he was carried away and found himself in Persian land. He inhabited a cave in the mountain of Senna where he founded a convent and there


[p. 449] {#po.13.p449}
هناك عمراً ورسم فيه قوانين تصلح لتدبير اولاده. واتصل خبره بالرهبان فإجتمعوا اليه من كل موضع. وشكى اليه يزدين بن ساطا! سن معاملة عامل باجرمى. فدفع اليه القديس حنانا وامره ان يكون معه اذا دخل اليه فانه يتخلص من مكروهه. وفمل ذاك فلقى العامل فاظهر ميلا اليه وترك عنه ما كان يطالبه به من الخراج» ووقع على هذا القديس فى بض الايام الشياطين لحندهم فحلقوا شعن "راسه ولحيته وكتفوه وضربو ضر شديدا وعلفوئ على شجرة حتى وجدود الرهبان فانزلوا به. وكان الرهبان يشاهدونه بالليل يصَلى وهو مثل السكران سقط كسلا من على كتفيه وعصالا من يدا وهو لا يحش بذلك. واجتاز رجل من وجوه المومنين يتقلّد اعمال طزيق دجلة فاقام بالعمسن ايم وشاهد ما يعمله هذا القدّيس وما يظبن على يديه. فلما اراد الانصراف لم بحب تركه
established rules for the direction of his children. The monks, having heard of him, went to him from all parts. Yazdin, son of Sata, having complained to the Saint of the bad treatment of the governor of Beith Garmai (Badjarmi), the latter gave him some hnana, advising him to carry it on him when he appeared before the governor, so that he might be sheltered from his injustices. Yazdin did so; the governor showed him sympathy and exempted him from the taxes he was demanding of him. The jealous demons once swooped down on him, and after having shaved his head and beard, they bound him, struck him hard, and tied him to a tree. He remained thus until the monks, having caught sight of him, brought him down. The monks saw him spending the nights in prayer and transported out of himself like a drunken man, so that his cloak fell from his shoulders and his staff from his hands, without him noticing it. One of the notables of the Christians, governor of the provinces crossed by the Tigris, passed by the convent, where he remained a few days. Having seen the works of the Saint and the miracles that he performed, he did not want to leave him there, but at the moment of his departure, he forced him to come down with him. The Saint, who was already


[p. 450] {#po.13.p450}
هناك فانزل به.ولا يعلم ما يزيد به المومن لعلو سن وسلامته. وقصد به عمن صلينا بهن صرص. لان العامل كان من ذلك الموضع. فاشتهى ان يكون القديس فيه. فلما راى ما تم عليه اغتعٌ واقام فى العس مدة. وقصدد المرضى فابراهم. ثم مات وعمر« ماية وعشرون سنة. ودفن فى هيكل عمر صلياء م راى تلميذه فى المنام دفعات القديس بخاطيه وقول. اتقلنى وردّنى الى عمرى. فصار الى قير ليلا وكان التلميذ فنكانً!. واخذ التابوت وجعله فى قلايته واعاد القبر صحبحً الى حالته ولم يعلم به احد. ووجدؤ صحيحا لم يتغير وله منذ استناح سنتان. ثم احتال حتى حمله الى سفينة رجل من اهل الكريخ بس من راى. فلما وصل الى الموضع وقف اهله على خض القديس وذلوا لامير اليلد شيًا وسالونا ان ياخذه من تلميذ وبدقنه فى بيعتم. واتصل الخ باهل السنّ فمنعوا من ذلك. ووقدت ينهم خصومة عظيمة الى ان
very advanced in age and simple of heart, without knowing what this man wanted of him, accompanied him to the convent of Sliba located on the bank of the Serser. This was indeed the country of this official, who expressed his desire to see the Saint inhabit this convent. The Saint, although afflicted by this way of acting, remained some time at the convent, healing the sick who came to find him. He died at the age of one hundred and twenty years and was buried in the temple of the convent of Sliba. The Saint appeared afterward several times in a dream to his disciple, saying to him: "Transport me from here to my convent." The disciple, who was the sacristan, went at night to his tomb, removed the coffin, and carried it into his cell. Having put the tomb back in order, as it was, no one noticed it. The Saint was intact and yet he had been dead for two years. The disciple then managed to transport him to a ship, which belonged to a man from Karkha of Surra-Man-Raa. When the body of the Saint arrived there, the people of the country, having learned of the Saint's affair, gave presents to the head of the province, begging him to take the remains of the Saint from his disciple and to bury them in their church. The inhabitants of Senna, having had news of this, opposed it. A violent quarrel flared up between them. Then an inhabitant of Tikrit intervened


[p. 451] {#po.13.p451}
توشطهم رجل من اهل تكريت على انه يجعل التابوت فى زوردق وف فى المآ بغبر ملاح. فان انحدر الى اسفل غلم ان الله اختار ان يدفن بكرب سس من راى. وان اصعد على انه يحب ان يدفن فى السن فى عمرا. فلما حصل اتابوت فى الزورق اصعد الى فوق المكم والما يجذبه الى اسفل وهو يصعد. تعيب الناس مما شاهدوا. وقدّمت السفينة الى تكريت واصعد بلتابوت الى البيعة هناك. وتبرك الناس منه. تم حمل الى عمر ودفن فيه»
and advised them to put the coffin in a small boat, which they would launch into the water and abandon to itself, without a boatman. If the boat came to go down the river, it would be a sign that God had chosen the city of Surra-Man-Raa for the burial of the Saint; if on the contrary the boat came to go up the river, it would be evident that he had to be buried in his convent of Senna. The boat, as soon as it had received the coffin, went up the waters, despite the current that struggled against it to make it go down. At this sight, all the assistants were stupefied. They brought the boat to shore at Tikrit, where they brought the coffin up to the church of this city, so that the people might receive its blessing. Then it was carried to his
٠ ولما كثر اللصوص فى الجيل ولم يمكن اناس العبور اليه بنوا عمراً الى جانب المديئة ونقلوا تابوت القديس اليه. وانتقل بعض الرهبان الى العس الجديد وقى بعضم فى الارّلة
convent, where it was deposited. When the brigands multiplied in the mountain and it was no longer possible for anyone to cross it, they founded another convent next to the city (of Senna), where they transported the remains of the Saint. Some monks went to inhabit the new convent; the others remained in the old one.

### XLVII. - History of Rabban Guiwarguis
ضر ربن جيورجيس هذا القديس رفيق برعاتا وخرج من العس بخروحه. واقام فى جبل حزة يتقوّت الثمار والحشائش. ووقف الرعاة على نخرلا وذكرود للناس. وابتداً ييرى الامراض.
This Saint was a companion of Bar 'Edta. He left with him from the convent (of Mar Abraham) and settled in the mountain of Adiabene, feeding on fruits and herbs. The shepherds, having seen his way of life, spoke


[p. 452] {#po.13.p452}
واجتمع اليه جماعة. فينى عمر ورسم فيه قوائين مار ابراهم. ودفع اليه المومنون الذين أبرا مرضاهم ضياع للعمر ثم راى لما قرت وفاته! ان رينى عمر بارض المرج. فمضى الى الموضع ونقل قوم من البراطقة عن اعتقادهم ممن سكن تلك القرى. ونى هناك عمرا. واجتمع فيه الرهان» ل خض رين يونا صاحب عمس انحل
of him to the people of the country. It was then that he began to heal the sick. He founded a monastery for those who had come to gather around him; he established there the rule of Mar Abraham. The faithful of the country whose sick he had healed gave him farms for his convent. Seeing the hour of death approach, he resolved to found another convent in the land of Marga. He went there; he converted many heretics, who lived in these villages, and built a convent where the monks gathered.

### XLVIII. - History of John (Yohanna), founder of the convent of Anhel
فى هذ المدة خرج هذا القديس من عمر مار ابراهم بسب خلف جرى ينه وين الاخوة على كر منه. وقصد بلد ارزن واقام بالقرب من قرية يقال لها انحل. ونى فى .يت كان للاصنام عمرا. واجتمع اليه الرهبان واظبر معجزات منها تحويله الخل خماً. وكات فيه خلنان بلغ هما هذ الاحوال صبن شديد وتواضع كثير. ولما شاخ مات ودفن فى عملا وحرف بعس رين .يوحنا انحل©
At this time, this Saint left the convent of Mar Abraham, because of a dispute that had broken out, to his great regret, between the brothers and him; he went to the land of Arzun and settled near a village called Anhel, where he transformed a temple of idols into a monastery. The monks gathered around him; he performed miracles; he once changed vinegar into wine. He was endowed with great patience and profound humility: two virtues, which made him arrive at this state (of perfection). He died old and was buried in his convent, which is known by the name of the monastery of Rabban John of Anhel.


[p. 453] {#po.13.p453}

### XLIX. - History of Rabban Haia
خرن رن حايا كان هذا الرجل من اهل كشكر. ولما اتصل به خير مار ابراهم عنق عبيده وفرّق ماله وقصده. وبل الرهينة منه. وقبله احد عبيده وترهب معه. واقاما فى العمر الى ان مات مار ابراهم. ثم مضى ربّن حايا الى بريّة مصر. وصلى فى بيت المقدس. ودعته النعمة الالبيه الى ان يعود الى ارض النبط وبي عمراً بارض كشكر فقعل ذلك واتصل ضر فضله بريّن سابونز صاحب عم تستر. فقصد واقام عند مد ومنعه من قصد مار ابراهم. فقبل منه وعفه! القوائين الى وضعها مار ابراهم للرهبان ونيخا. ثم أنصرف الى عملا بنستر. ونى بن حايا العس واجتمع اليه الرهبان. وكان بينه وين تيادوروس الذى عمل الاسكول بشنت موانسة. وطالت حياة ربمن حايا وهذا اعجب كون نير اسمه يعيش. ولما استناح دفن فى عمر الذى بلا صلواته تكون مغينة لنا شافعة.
This man was from Kashkar. Having heard of Mar Abraham, he freed his slaves, distributed his goods, and went to him. He received from his hands the monastic habit, and settled in the convent with one of his slaves, who had become a monk with him. After the death of Mar Abraham, Rabban Haia went to the desert of Egypt (Misr) and prayed in Jerusalem (Beit el-Magdis). Divine grace then called him to return to Aramaic land (An-Nabat) and to build a convent in the region of Kashkar. He did so. His high reputation having reached the ears of Rabban Sabor, founder of the convent of Suetar (Toustar), the latter came to find him and spent some time with him. Rabban Haia prevented him from going to visit Mar Abraham; he made known to him the rules that the latter had established for the monks. He copied them and returned to his convent of Suetar. Rabban Haia founded the convent and gathered monks there. He had relations of friendship with Theodore, the founder of the School of Suetar. Rabban Haia lived a long time; a curious thing, indeed, his name means: he lives. After his death, he was buried in the convent that he had built. May his prayers be our help and our assistance.


[p. 454] {#po.13.p454}

### L. - History of Rabban Babai of Nisibis
خبر رين بابى التسيبيني فى هذه المدة كان هذا المضوط وهو من وجوا اهل نضيبين. وكان فى حدائته مستهتراً بالشرب وسماع الغنا واللحب فانتخه الله. فارى مام ازعجه فقصد مار ابراهم. فلما ران كشف له عن امرة وان يصير ابا لخلق كثير. وستلا بابى. وترهب. وصار فى حملة اولادة. فلما استناح! مار ابراهم تفتزد فى مغارة بجبل حر مع ايشوعزخا الخيط وسبريشوع الذّى صار جائليقا. وهما اللذان اخرجا الشيطان من النعمن ملك الحيرة. وكان ابشوعزخا يتعجب من سك بابى وتقشفه وسن دبار«3. وحمل فى بعض الاوقات الى ابشوعزخا وهو مقيم فى الين بحرة غلام مسترخى الرجلين. فلما وصاوا الى ناحية
It is to this period also that this Blessed one belongs. He was one of the notables of Nisibis (Nasibin). In his youth, he had given himself over with dissipation to drinking, songs, and gambling. God, who wanted to choose him, sent him a vision, which troubled him. He went to Mar Abraham, who, having seen him, predicted his destiny and announced to him that he would be a Father to a great number of men. He gave him the name of Babai and received him into the number of his children by giving him the monastic habit. After the death of Mar Abraham, he retired into a cave in the mountain of Adiabene (Hazza) with the anchorite Isho'zkha and Sabrisho', who became catholicos. It is these last two who drove the demon out of En-Nu'man, king of Hira. Isho'zkha admired the devotion of Babai, his mortifications, and his good works. One day when Isho'zkha was absent and was in a convent in Adiabene (Hazza), they brought to the latter a lame child (to heal him). When they arrived near the cave of Mar Babai, the father of the child sat down to rest from the fatigue of the road and to eat something. The camels having escaped, they went down in search of them.


[p. 455] {#po.13.p455}
مغارة مر بابى جلس والد الغلام هناك ليستريحوا من العنا الذى لحتهم فى الطريق وتكلوا شيئا. وضلت جمليم ونزلوا الى اسقل فى طلبها. فبكى الصبى ليعدهم عنه. وسمعة من بإلى فخرج من مغارته وساله عن خرلا. فعّفه ان بكلا لاجل بعد والديه عنه. فامرا ان يمضى فى طلبهما. فذكر له انه لا يمكنه ” القيام على رجليه. فاخذ بيده واقامه ورجع ابواه فنظرلا قائماً. وطلبا انسانا اقامه فلم يجدوا ومضوا الى ايشوعزخا وعرفوه خبرهم. قال ليس الملاك وهب لانكم رحليه كما تظنّون. لكن مار بابى النصييبى المقيم فى المغارة المجاورة للقرية المعروفة ببيت الجيال»
The child, seeing himself all alone, began to cry. Mar Babai, whom the cries of the child had made come out of his cave, asked him why he was crying. He replied that he was crying because his parents had just left him. The Saint told him to join them; but the child let him know that he could not stand up. Then he took him by the hand and made him stand on his feet. When his parents, upon their return, saw him standing and could not see, despite their searches, a human being who had healed him, they went to see Isho'zkha to make their story known to him. "It is not the angel," he told them, "who healed the feet of your child, as you think, but it is Mar Babai of Nisibis, who inhabits the cave that is near the village called Beith Gammala."
وكان صر هذا القديس على مقاومة الشياطين يجوز الوصف. وصاروا اليه في بمض الاتام فشدوا فى عنقه حلا وعلقولا على خشبة فوق كرحه3. ولم يمكنهم الله من اتلافه. وحرهم بكلمة الله فهربوا وتركود. ولحقه الاخوة فحملود. وعمل ايات كثيرة. وعد مدة طويلة ارى ان يبثى عمرا ففعل. وناد فى جيل الازل ورسم فيه قوانين مار ابراهم.
The patience of this Saint in enduring the attacks of the demons was inexpressible. One day, they threw themselves upon him, tied a rope around his neck, and fastened him to a piece of wood at his cave. But God did not give them the power to destroy him. The Saint having adjured them by the word of God, they left him and took flight; the brothers came and delivered him || [he] performed many miracles. Long after, he deemed it opportune to found a convent in the mountain of Izla (Al-Izl); he established there the rule of Mar Abraham. He survived its foundation for thirty-one years; after his death, his remains were interred in his convent. We have spoken of this in detail in his biography


[p. 456] {#po.13.p456}
وعاش بعد بنائه العمس احدى وثلثين سنة ومات. ودفن فى عما. وقد شرحا اخباره فى تشعيث! عملت له صلواته تحفطنا وتنجناة
which I composed myself. May his prayers preserve us and grant us salvation.

### LI. - History of Rabban Yaunan
خر ربّن يونان كان لبعض المجوس مملوك اسمه يونان وكان يواصل المضى الى من بابى فى مغارته يساله عن اشيا: وانفذ مولا فى بعض الاوقات الى حبل حَرةٍ ليحمل شرابا من كرم له. فعدل الى مغارة مر بابى. فاقام عند وتاخّر عما أمرة به مولالا وقزع منه. وذكن لمن بابى خرا فامر ان يملاً الزقاق ما من تلك العين التى تجرى بين يدى مثارّه وحمليا. ففعل يونان ذلك. فلقيه مولاه فى الطريق. وذاق .الما فوجده خم جِيّدً. م وقفا المجوسى على قصة الغلام فعتقه. ومضى الى مر بابى واقام معه يخدمه وتعلم شرائع النصرانيّة
A magus had a slave named Yaunan. The latter often went to visit Mar Babai in his cave to converse with him. One day, his master sent him to the mountain of Adiabene (Hazza) to bring him wine from his vineyard. The slave headed toward the cave of Mar Babai and stopped with him; and he was delayed in the execution of his master's order. He was then afraid of his master and confided in Mar Babai. The latter ordered him to fill the skins with water at the fountain that flowed in front of his cave and to bring it (to his master). Yaunan obeyed. His master, having met him on the road, drank of this water, which he found to be an excellent wine. Informed then of his slave's story, he set him free. The slave went to Mar Babai and remained with him to serve him; he was instructed in the precepts of Christianity and became a monk. When Mar Babai moved to the convent he built, Yaunan remained in the cave;


[p. 457] {#po.13.p457}
وترهب. فلما بنى مر بابى العمر انتقل اليه. واقام يونان فى المغادة. واتصّل خبرة الرهبان. فقصدوه وسكنوا حوله ونى فى الموضع عمر بالقرب من قرية تعرف بباطى ورسم فيه مر بابى! ومات ودفن فيه»
and, in order to preserve there the tradition of Mar Babai, and to house the monks who, upon learning his story, had come to group themselves around him, he built on the site, very close to a village called Bati, a convent, where he died and was interred.

### LII. - History of Sahrona
شر تهرونا فى هذه المدة ظهر امر هذا المضوط. وكان من وجول نصيبين. وقصد مار ابراهم ول منه الزهينة. ومبكث ارسين سنة حبيساً يصبر على الح والبرد ولا ياكل الخبز فى التوابيع الثلث. ثم ارى ان يقصد ارض قردا وبنى بها عمراً. ففعل ذلك. واجتمع اليه الرهبان. ثم مات ودفن فيه»
This Blessed one, who also belongs to this era, was one of the notables of Nisibis. He went to Mar Abraham and received the monastic habit from his hands. After having led the life of an anchorite for forty years, enduring heat and cold and depriving himself of bread during the three weeks, he went to the lands of Qardu, where he built a convent for the monks who gathered around him. After his death, he was buried in this convent.


[p. 458] {#po.13.p458}
خير مر يهب! التخيط فى هذا الزمان ظهر هذا القديس. واتصل به خن دانيال تلميذ مار ابراهم المقيم بكشكر. فقصده. ثم اقام عنده شهراً. ثم فارقه ومضى الى الزاب الاكبر. فاقام فى مغارة بالقرب منه. ورا الصيادون دفعات وقد رسم على المآ صليناً ومشى عليه. وسمعوه وهو يقول فى صلاته يا مسيح قتلتنى محّتك. وبدان مبسوطتان الى السماء ودموعه تجرى. واتصل خا بيزدين النى لد كسرى اعمال كسرة من بلاد النبط والججل. فساله فى امن ابن له مات وطرحه على باب مغارته. وما زال يطلب منه ان رسال الله هبته له. فصلّى ودعا وسمع الله منه. وقام الشّبى ودفعه الى ابيه كما فعل ابليشع بابن الارملة السلومية. ثم هرب من مغارته وكشف له سيدنا المسيح لذكرلا السجود خب امراة نخريطة
LIII. - History of Mar Yahb (Yahna) the anchorite. This Saint belongs to this era. Having heard of Daniel, a disciple of Mar Abraham who lived in Kashkar, he came to find him and remained with him for a month. Then he separated from him and went to dwell in a cave near the Great Zab. The fishermen often saw him making the sign of the cross over the waters and walking on the river; and they heard him saying in his prayer, with arms raised to heaven and eyes full of tears: "O Christ! Thy love has killed me." Yazdin, to whom Khosrow (Kisra) had entrusted the government of several provinces of Beth Aramaye (Nabat) and the Mountain, having heard of him, came and insistently asked him to pray to God to restore life to one of his sons, who was dead, and he laid him before the door of his cave. The Saint prayed much; God heard him; for the child arose and the Saint returned him to his father, as Elisha had returned her son to the Shunammite widow. Then he left his cave. Our Lord Christ (may his name be adored) revealed to him the retreat of an anchorite woman who lived on a mountain; he went to visit her


[p. 459] {#po.13.p459}
مقيمة فى بعض الجال. فصار اليها وتبرك ها. وذكر ان قميصها كان من كولان البردى وغذاها من عقاقير الجل. فلما مات دفن فى مغارته. ثم نقل يزدين حسدا بعد اديع وعشرين سنة. ودفنه مع الشهدا. فى كرخ حدان. وله رسالة مختصرة يدل فها على الخالق عر وجل والخليقة. وقد عمل تفسيرها افنى ماران رئيس عمس الرزعفران صلواته معناه خير دين سابور.
to obtain her blessing. He recounted that the recluse's shirt was made of papyrus film and that her food was the roots of the mountain plants. He was buried after his death in the cave he inhabited. Twenty-four years later, Yazdin transported his remains to Karkha of Guedan and placed them in the tomb of the martyrs. He wrote a letter in which he treats succinctly of the Creator (may his name be exalted) and of the creatures. It was commented upon by Apnimaran, superior of the convent of Za'faran. May his prayers be with us.

### LIV. - History of Rabban Sabor
ظهن امر هذا القديس وهو من اهل الاهواز من قرية تعرف بالدولاب. وكان اصغر اخوته. وحسدوا لانه رسم على خنطة بسيرة كانت له صليبا. فاكتفوا بها الى وقت الله من التمنة المقبلة. واجتبى من صغزلا مثل سمئيل وارميا ويونًا. وتعلّم وصار ملفانً! فى
This Saint was a native of Khuzistan (Al-Ahwaz), from a village called Ad-Dolab. His brothers, of whom he was the youngest, were envious of him because one time, by the sign of the cross, he had multiplied a little wheat he had, so that it had sufficed for them for a whole year until the first harvest. He was chosen from his childhood, like Samuel, Jeremiah, and John the Baptist. After completing his studies, he was for some time a doctor


[p. 460] {#po.13.p460}
اسكول! دير محراق مدة. ثم ضار الى تستر مع سبعة نفر صحولا من الاسكولتّين. وتدبّر دبّار القديسين. ثم صاروا الى ربن حايا الى كشكر. فعرنهم طرقة الرهبان ودفع الهم والى رب سابور قوانين مار ابراهم. وعاد الى موضعة ونى عمراً فى جيل تستر. واجتمع اليه خلى كثير وتلمذ الاكراد المجاورين. وعاش الى ايام ايشوعيب الجائليق الاخير. ولقيه وقله وساله وقال ممن قبلت الرجينة. فقال له من قوانين مار ابراهم ومن ربّن حايا. ولما صار يوحنًا بن مريّا الذى صار حاثليقا واسحق الذى صار اسقف كريخ السوس وسورين الذى صار اسقف مهرجا الى عمر ذبن سابور قال لهم هذا القديس. ان الله قد اجتباكم رعاة لبيعته. وعد قليل تدعوكم النعمة. فاحنوا القيام بما توهلون له. وكان الاس على ما ذكرل لهم.
in the school of Deir-Mihraq. Then he went to Suetar (Tustar), accompanied by seven students, who led a holy life with him. They soon went to see Rabban Haia in Kashkar, who taught them the monastic life and gave to them and to Rabban Sabor the rule of Mar Abraham. (Rabban Sabor) returned to the country from which he came and built a convent in the mountain of Suetar. Many people gathered around him. He converted the neighboring Kurds (Al-Akrad). He lived until the time of Ishoyahb, the last catholicos of that name. The latter saw him, received him, and asked him: "Who taught you the monastic life?" "The rule," he replied, "of Mar Abraham, and Rabban Haia." When John (Yohanna), son of Marta, who became catholicos, Isaac, who was consecrated bishop of Karkha of Susa, and Sourin, who was bishop of Mihradja, went to the convent of Rabban Sabor: "God," this Saint said to them, "has chosen you to be shepherds in his Church; grace will soon call you. Accomplish well that of which you will be worthy." His word was fulfilled to the letter.
ظهر فى بعض الايام تنين اعظم ما يكون بخرج منه مثل شعاع النّار. فحرمه بكلمة
One day (there appeared in the region) an enormous serpent, from whose mouth came sparks like fire. The Saint having adjured it by the word of God, it became (inert) like a chain stretched from the summit of the


[p. 461] {#po.13.p461}
الله فصار مثل السلسلة من راس الجبل والى اسفله يتعجب منه كلمن يرلا الى وقننا هذا. ثم مات رين سابور ودفن فى عمرا قدام البيكل. ومن تلاميذه ربّن خذاهو صاحب عمر يث حالا وبلكيشوع صاحب الحديث بجندسابور وابشوع عتنه صاحب عمر فانكان!. صلاتهم اجمع تكون مناه ِ خبن موريقى ملك الروم.
mountain to its foot: which is still seen today and plunges all visitors into astonishment. Then Rabban Sabor died and was interred in his convent before the temple. Among his disciples, we cite Rabban Khoudawi, the founder of the convent of Beth Hale, Malkiso', the founder of 'Oumra Hadtha at Gundishapur, and Le'ommeh, the founder of the convent of Falikan. May the prayers of all (these saints) be with us.

### LV. - History of Maurice, Emperor of the Greeks
قد تقتم بان طياربوس الملك على الروم مات فى سنة اديع وسعين وثمان مائة للاسكندر. ولم يخلف ولد يرث الملك. كان مورقى زوج .بنته وصاحب “ حيشه. فوصى ان يكون الملك بعدد. فملك المملكة بعدد. وكان خيّر فاضلًا. فدب الملك احسن تدير. وظفره الله بسائر من طلبه من الاعدا وملا ببوت امواله مالا. واظبر الاعتقاد بالجوهرين فى المسيح. وكاتب اصحابه بنفى من .يخالف هذا .الاعتقاد. واخلى الليع منهم "رحمة الله»
We have already said that Tiberius (Tibaryous), Emperor of the Greeks, who died in the year 894 of Alexander, left no son to succeed him. Maurice (Mouriqi), husband of his daughter and general of his armies, whom he had designated as his successor, reigned after him. He was good and virtuous. He governed the empire with wisdom. God made him triumph over all the enemies who attacked him; he filled the state treasuries with silver. He publicly confessed the duality of natures in Christ and wrote to his governors to exile those who opposed this belief: thus the churches were rid of heretics. May God have mercy on him.


[p. 462] {#po.13.p462}
خر رين يعقوب صاحب عمر بيث عابا هذا القديس من مدينة لاشوم من عمل باجرمى. وكان ابلا واهله يتصرفون فى خدمة كسرى. فسعى هم اليه. فنهب امواليم وحبس عمه. فهرب هذا الى ارض المرج سيب لانتخاب الله ايد كما خرج موسى وحقوب وبوسف عن اهاليهم. وترك ماله ولزم التعلّم فى الاسكول هناك الى ان ظهر فى هذ المدة. وكان حِميلًا بيا. ثم صار ملفان الاسكول ثمان سنين. وتلمذ هناك خلقاً ونقل عن عاد الاصنام كثيراً. واتصل به خبر عمر مار ابراهم فقصده واقام فيه مع بابى الكبير وابى النَصِى وداذيشوع ورفقاتهم سبع سنين فى كرح مفرد. ثم خرج عنه وهو كاره لما احبّه الله من علو شانه وارتفاع أمرن. وصحبه سبعة نفر من الرهبان من كل موضع. وتلمذ له
LVI. - History of Rabban Jacques, the founder of the convent of Beth 'Abe. This Saint was a native of the city of Lasom in the province of Beth Garmai (Badjarmi). His father and family were in the service of Khosrow; but detractors slandered them to the prince, who confiscated their goods and threw Jacques's uncle into prison. Jacques (Ya'qoub), whom God wished to choose, abandoned his goods and went to Marga (Al-Mardj), thus expatriating himself like Moses, Jacob, and Joseph. He applied himself to study in the school of that country; his fame then spread. He was handsome and radiant of face. Then he was a doctor of the school for eight years. He converted several (heretics) and turned many people away from idolatry. Having heard of the convent of Mar Abraham, he went there and remained for seven years with Babai the Great, Babai of Nisibis, Dadiso', and their companions. || [He] lived in an isolated cell. He then left it, despite his desire, fearing the glory and greatness to which God was calling him. He was accompanied by seven monks from different regions. Ishoyahb of Adiabene, who became catholicos, became his disciple. He did not sleep once since


[p. 463] {#po.13.p463}
ايشوعيب الحزى الذى صار حاثليقاً. ومنذ ترهب لم ينم. وكان اذا احس يفلبه النّوم قام الى الّلاة. وتزيّد فى التعليم من حرفور المشهور بالفضل مطران_ نصيين. وصار اليه فى بعض الاوقات ثلث نسو عواقر يسالنه الصلاة ليرزقن اولادً. فاخذ خرقة صغيرة وجدها فى كرحه. فختها ثلث قطع ودفع الى كل واحدة مني قطعة. فلما كان فى السنة المقبلة صرن اليه ومعهن ثلث بنين. ودفعن الى العمر بركة وافرة. وعمر هذا القديسن طوتا الى اخر ايام كسرى الاخير. فلما استتاح دفن فى عملا وساتى به© خبر الس الذى كان يذبح للشيطان وهى قصة ظريفة
he became a monk; if he felt overcome by sleep, he would rise to pray. || [He] surpassed in his teaching Gregory (Djrighour), metropolitan of Nisibis, a man famous for his virtues. Once, three sterile women came to ask him to pray for them so that they might have children. He took a small piece of cloth he found in his cell, cut it into three pieces, and gave one to each of them. A year later, they came to see him, each carrying their son, and made great gifts to the convent. This Saint lived a long time, until the last days of Khosrow (Kisra), the last. After his death, he was buried in his convent, which was called by his name. LVII. - Small anecdote of the priest who sacrificed to the demon.
كان بارض بانهئرا مدينة يقال ليا فيروز شابور اعلبا تنا وكان لبا اسقف مفرد اسمه
There was in the region of Beth Nuhadra a city called Piroz-Sabor—unhappy its inhabitants! There was an isolated bishop there, named


[p. 464] {#po.13.p464}
حزفيال وكان ها رجل اسكلانى! يقال له اسحق لا بحن شي فخرج عنها ومضى الى حزّان. ثم رجع الها وقد ظهر علمه وفهمه. فاستحسن الناس كلامه فجعلوة مقشرا فى اسكول هذه المديئة. وكان يخدم الشياطين وقرب لبا القرابين سر]: فلما حضر الصوم المارانى طالله الشيطان الذى يخدمه ان يذبح له على عادته. ققدم اسح على قعله وداقعه طول الصوم. فلما كان .يوم الشعانين حِدٌ فى مطليته بالقريان له. فساله ان بو ذلك اليوم. ودافعه وحم يوم خميس الفصح وقد استظهن اسحق قصة العيد واراد الخروج الى الشب ليقراها. فعلق به صاحبه وقال له ان لم تذبح لى فى هذا اليوم قتلتك. فاجتهد به ان يوخ فلم يفعل. وقال له قد رفغت منزلتك وجاك رين بعد ان كنت وضيعًا حقيراً. وتعدني من اول الصوم وتخدعنى. وعندك دحاحتان فان ذبحتهما لى والا قتلتك. فلم يزل يساله وضرع اليه فى اماله. فلم يبل وضريه على راسه قط وسمع الاسكولائبون صوته فادروا اليه فوجدوة مطروحا. فشرح ليم خب واستحلفهم آلا يدفنوا بصلاة بل
Ezekiel (Hazqyyal), and a student named Isaac (Ishaq), who did not know many things. The latter left it and went to Harran. When he returned to his country, he was found to be learned and intelligent, and his speech was much appreciated; thus he was charged with being an interpreter in the city's school. But he served the demons and sacrificed to them in secret. As Lent approached, the demon he served demanded that he sacrifice to him according to his custom. Isaac, who repented of his conduct, resisted throughout Lent. On Palm Sunday, the demon made every effort to force him to sacrifice. But he still refused and asked him for a delay. On Easter day, Isaac having wished to appear before the people in order to deliver a homily he had prepared for the feast, his friend (the demon) clung to his person and said to him: "If you do not offer me a sacrifice today, I will kill you." Isaac tried to obtain a delay; it was in vain. "I raised you to your position," the devil said to him, "and I established you as a master, you who were inferior and despised; and you, since the beginning of Lent, have been deceiving me with your promises. You have two hens; sacrifice them to me; otherwise, I will kill you." Isaac implored him insistently, asking for a delay. The devil refused and dealt him a blow on the head, which made him fall to the ground. The students, who heard his cry, rushed over and found him


[p. 465] {#po.13.p465}
يطرحونه من الجبال. فلما مات فعلوا به ذلك كما سال. خلّصنا الله تعالى من مكائد الشطان ورزقنا النجاة من شاكه» ا لض ري ابرويز بن هربز
stretched out on the ground. He told them what had happened and adjured them not to bury him with ceremonies, but to throw him from the top of the mountain. When he was dead, they treated him as he had requested. May God the Most High deliver us from the ruses of the demon and pull us from his nets!

### LVIII. - History of Khosrow Parwez, son of Hormizd
لما خلع العسكر هرمز لقبح معاملته ليم وتحصّل وجوهيم فى الحبوس واطعاميم الخز المخلوط فيه الج كاتبوا ابنه كسرى ابروز فى العود الى المدائن وهو انه من اخته. وكان قد هرب منه. وعاد وعقدوا له المملكة عليهم. وكان حدث الس فم بالنجوم عاملًا يا متكلا عليها معجً منبّا للاكل والشرب والتسا. وكان ابو ينغصه. فلما تم له الامر تشاغل لبو واطلق من كان فى الحبوس ورد عليهم ما كان ابو اخذد منهم. فس به الناس.
When the army dethroned Hormizd because of his bad treatment and the imprisonment of the notables, to whom he had bread mixed with plaster given, they wrote to his son Khosrow Parwez (Kisra Abrawiz), who had been born to him from his marriage with his sister and who was in flight, to return to Seleucia (Al-Madain). He returned and was proclaimed king. He was young, vain, fond of good food, a drinker, and a libertine. He was also versed in astrology, to which he devoted himself and gave all his trust. His father kept him severely. Once in possession of power, he gave free rein to his frivolous tastes. He released the prisoners and returned to them what his father had
وسكث مديدة وهرب برام شوبين العاصى عليه بعد ان كان السبب فى ابقاع الوحثة ينه وين ابيه والحسكر وقاتله وغلبه على المملكة وح الناس بلايا عظيمة. وانبزم ابرويز وقصد موريقى ملك الروم.معتضد به. وكتب اليه يما جرى عليه وساله الاخذ بيده وانفاذ
taken from them. Everyone rejoiced at it. Bahram Sobin (Sobin) soon found himself obliged to take flight. (Here is the story of this Bahram). After having thrown the army into turmoil, and having been


[p. 466] {#po.13.p466}
ارعين الف مقاتل وارسين ققطار ذهب للنفقة على الجيش. فاحابه مورشى الى ما ساله وله كما يجب عليه فى ديثه ووه اليه ما طلبه. واتفذ مع الجيش قائدين الى قريسيا. وكتب اليه بالمصين الى عانة او نصيبين. فانهما الحدّان بين المملكتين.
the cause of the disagreement between Khosrow and his father, Bahram entered into rebellion against Khosrow, made war on him, and seized his throne, which caused great misfortunes to befall the people. Parwez took flight and took refuge with Maurice (Mouriqi), Emperor of the Greeks, to implore his help. He wrote to him everything that had happened to him and begged him to help him by sending him forty thousand combatants and forty quintals of gold for the expenses of the army. Maurice granted his request and received him as is fitting for a man full of religion. He granted him what he had asked for. He sent with the troops
فصار كسرى الى الرها واكرمه من لقيه من الروم فى طرقه. ثم سار الى منبج فاجتمع مع الجيش المنفذ اليه. وعاد كسرى الى العراق وقاتل يرام وقزمه الى اليل وطلبه الروم فهرب الى بلاد الترك. واستولى كسرى على الملك ووصل حيوش الروم بصلات حليلة كثيرة. وانفذ الى مورقى هدايا نفيسة. وسمّلا ايلا وتزوج بابنته مريم. وقال ان اسمها شيرين. ونزل له عن دارا وميافارقين وكان ابو هرمز قد تغلب عليهما. وامر كسرى بتجديد البيع وكرام التصارى بسبب مورقى ونا لمريم يعتين ولشييين
two generals as far as Circesium and wrote to him to go to 'Ana or else to Nisibis: for these two cities were situated on the borders of the two empires. Khosrow went to Edessa (Ar-Roha); he was received with honor by the Greeks he met on his way; he then arrived at Mabboug (Manbedj), where he met the troops that were given to him. Khosrow returned to 'Iraq, where he defeated Bahram, who fled into the mountain. The Greeks having pursued him, he took refuge in the land of the Turks. Khosrow was master of the empire. He gave the Greek troops precious and magnificent gifts; he also sent precious gifts to Maurice, whom he called his father; he married his daughter Mary—others say she was called Sirin—and ceded to him Dara and Mipargat, which his father Hormizd had seized. Khosrow, out of gratitude to Maurice, ordered the churches to be rebuilt and the Christians to be honored. He himself built two churches for Mary (his wife), and a large church and a castle in the land of Beth


[p. 467] {#po.13.p467}
امراته الى من بلد النّط يعة كبيرة وقصرا فى بلد بلاسفر. وما زال الصارى معه فى حملة العلامة الى لض ايام من سبريشوع الجاثليق بعد ايشوعيب وموت مورشى. رحميم الله خب القس الذى تسج
Laepar for his wife Sirin, the Aramean. In his time, the Christians enjoyed peace, until the last days of Mar Sabriso', who succeeded Ishoyahb, and until the death of Maurice. May God have mercy on them.

### LIX. - History of the priest who had become a magus
كان فى ايلم كسرى هذا قش قد كفر بالنصرانّة من شت محّته للدنيا ورغيته فى ملاذها. وصار مجوستًا. فاكريه الملك ورفع منه. ثم احب ان يمتحنه ليعلم انتقاله لئيّة صحيحة او رغبة فى الدنيا. فقال له اشتهى ان ارى كيف يعمل النصارى الرازين!. فلتمس القس ان يفرز له ينا وجل فيه ما يحتاج اليه من آلة القريان. فتقدم الملك بذلك واحضر له سائ ما طلبه. وجلس الملك فى موضع ينظر ما يعمله. وابتدا القس يقدّس. فلما بلغ وقت نزول روح القدس راى الملك البيت مملوً ملائكة عليهم لباس يضى مثل الس
In the time of this Khosrow, there was a priest whom a lively attachment to the world and its pleasures had led to abjure Christianity to embrace the religion of the Magi. The king, who had honored him and raised him in dignity, then wished to test him with the goal of knowing if it was by conviction or for the love of the world that he had changed religion: "I desire," he said to him, "to see how the Christians celebrate the mysteries." The priest asked him to place at his disposal an isolated house, with all the objects required for the celebration of the sacrifice. The king ordered everything he asked for to be prepared for him, and he sat in a place from where he could see what the priest was going to do. The latter began to say the mass. At the moment of the descent of the Holy Spirit, the king saw the house full of angels, dressed in robes shining like the sun, and who were glorifying God


[p. 468] {#po.13.p468}
يمسحون الله باصوتهم. وقد نجلل" القش بمثل السنة النار والبس نور عثظيماً. اتتعجب كسرى مما جرى وبا شاهدة وحمد الله على ما اراد. ولما استتم القس القتاس خرج عاب من كل ما عليه. ققال له الملك لا نصرانياً انت ولا مجوسّا. ودث الى ايشوعيب الجائليق يعرفه ذلك وساله ما عنده فيما يفعل بالقش. فقال الام للملك ونهما رآ صوا فليفعل. فلس بصلبه واخذ ماله وزاد فى اكرام الصادى والنحة ليم رحمة الله»
by their songs; (and he saw) the priest resplendent with tongues of fire and dressed in a great light. Khosrow, struck by this spectacle, thanked God for the vision he had granted him. When the priest had finished the mass, he was seen stripped of what he was dressed in. "You are," the king said to him, "neither Christian nor magus." He made this known to the catholicos Ishoyahb, asking him how one should act with the priest: "It is for the king," he said, "to order and to do what will seem just to him." He ordered him to be crucified and his goods to be confiscated. From that day, he honored the Christians and loved them more. May God have mercy on him.

### LX. - History of an-Nu'man, son of al
ب النعمن بن المنذر ملك الحيرة.
Mundhir, king of Hira
كان هذا الرجل متمشكاً بدين الحنوفيّة مثل فولوس بالبهوديّة ومار. ابا بالمجوسّة يعبد الكوكب المعروف الزهرة وذبح الذبائح للاصنام. فولع به الشيطان. فاعتضد بكبنة الاصنام فلم ينفعوا. فلجا الى شمعون بن حابن اسقف الحيرة وسبريشوع اسقف لاشو الذى انتهت الجثلقة اليه وايشوعزخا الراهب. واعتضد بصلواتهم. وكذلك نحن. فوهب الله له العافية وخرج منه الشيطان. ١
Just as Paul had adhered to Judaism and Mar Aba to Magism, so this man was attached to the pagan religion. He worshipped the star called Zohra and offered sacrifices to the idols. The demon possessed him. It was in vain that he asked for help from the priests of the idols. He resorted to Simeon (Sem'oun), son of Jabir, bishop of Hira, to Sabriso', bishop of Laeom, who became catholicos, and to the monk Isho'zkha, and solicited their prayers, which we also solicit. God healed him; the demon left him.


[p. 469] {#po.13.p469}
واعتمذ وذلك فى السنة الراب لملك: كسرى. وحسن ايمانه وطرد البعقوتّة من: سائر أغماله. ونمسسك بالايمان التتحيح. وكان ملكا على سائر العرب فى مملكتى الفرس والروم والحال بين ملوكهم مستقيمة. وان احتاج اليه احدهما لم يتاخر عنه. وكذلك صار ولدط.
He received the faith and baptism in the fourth year of King Khosrow. He was attached to the sound belief; he drove the Jacobites from all his provinces in his zeal for orthodoxy. He reigned over all the Arabs who were in the two empires of the Persians and the Greeks. If one or the other of these two kings, who were then at peace, asked him for support, he hastened to come to his aid. His son acted likewise.
ولما راى الحسن والمنذر اباد النعمة التى حلت على ابيهما اعتمدا ابضا بعده سنة واعمدا اهل بيتهما. ونقدم الحسن الى عبيده الا يمنعوا المساكين عنه عند دخوله البيعة. وحيث خرج بسطام على كسرى قاومه الحسن وخلّصه من يدد. وقد كاد ان يحصل فيا. وكان الحسن اشد الجماعة تمتك بالنصرانيّة. رحمهم الله تعالى»
His sons Hassan and Mundhir, having seen the grace which their father enjoyed, also received baptism a year after him, and had all the people of their house baptized. Hassan ordered his slaves not to prevent the poor from approaching him when he entered the church. And when Bistam revolted against Khosrow, Hassan fought him and delivered Khosrow from his hands. But it was little short of Hassan falling there himself. He was the man most attached to the Christian religion. May God the Most High have mercy on them.

### LXI. - History of Yaunan, the founder of the convent of Bar Toura
خبر يونان صاحب عمر برطورا ١ “ هذا القديس من قرية بناحية باعربايا. وكان اكار. فاختار» الله تعالى وقصد النخارطة تلاميذ مار ابراهم المقيمين بجبل سنجار. فاقام متهم عشر سنين يزدع فى الل وصد
This Saint was born in a village in the region of Beth 'Arbaye (Ba'arbaia); he was a plowman when God the Most High chose him. He went to the


[p. 470] {#po.13.p470}
ويطحن وتقتّت باقوات القتيسين وتعلم منهم دبّار الرمبنة. وكشف ليم عن املا وانه انتخب لبناء عم فى ذلك الموضع. كان يونان هذا تلميذ احدهم. وشال له مار ايا ققال للباقين هذا هو الرجل الذى بعثه الله تعالى ليبنى فى هذا الججل عمرا عظيماً. ولما اراد مار ابا زاد فى التقشف وامتنع من كل شى. سوى ورق الصَّمر والحشائش. واظهر آيات كثيرة وفتح عين نمز قلعتها نمرة على باب كرحه. وتنا على بطلان ملك الفرس وظبور العرب وما يلحتى الناس من الشاتة. واجتمع اليه الرهبان لبنا العمر. وقال سيجى العرب ولد اسمعيل من البرّة وسلكون. وبخرب هذا العمر. وهدا شيع سنين يكون سكون ف العالم وتعودون الى العمر من حيث تبددتم وتجتمعون فيه. كم ومات. ودفن فى عملا وستّى عمز برطورا»
anchorites, disciples of Mar Abraham, who lived on the mountain of Sinjar (Sindjar); he spent ten years with them, occupying himself with sowing, harvesting, and grinding, feeding himself in the same way as them, and learning the monastic life from them. He revealed to them that God had chosen him to build a convent in that place. One of them, Mar Aba, whose disciple Yaunan had become, said to the others: "This is the man whom God the Most High has sent to found a great monastery in this mountain." When Mar Aba expressed his will to him, Yaunan entered into new mortifications || [he] deprived himself of all food except for thyme leaves and herbs; he performed many miracles; he opened the eyes of a tiger, whose eyes a tigress had gouged out, at the door of his cell; he predicted the end of the Persian empire, the appearance of the Arabs, and the misfortunes that men would have to endure. The monks gathered around him for the construction of the convent. "The Arabs, children of Ishmael," he said to them, "will come from the desert and will reign. This convent will be destroyed; but seven years later, peace will reign in the universe. Then you, who will have been dispersed, will return to this convent and will find yourselves reunited there." Then he blessed them and died. His convent, in which he was interred, was named the convent of Bar Toura.


[p. 471] {#po.13.p471}

### LXII. - History of Jacques, founder of the convent of Hbisa
خير يعقوب صاحب عمر الحبيس فى هذا الزمان ظهر هذا القديس بارض ارزن. وعمل ايات كثيرة. وقال انه اجتاز يوم بقوم يكسحون كرما. فاليم عما يعملون. فاستزروه بسبب ثيابه الالية وقالوا له مستهزئين به. نقلع كرمً ونغرس عوسجا. فاجاهم كما فل ابليشع النَى بالصّبيان الذين هزثوا به. وقال يكون مثلما قلتم. فبقى الكرم اعجو الى وقتنا لان خشبه يشه سفش! الكرم وودقه مثل ورق العوسج. ولما اتصل خرلا بالرهبان اجتمعوا اليه. ووجدوا فى الموضع الذى كان فيه رجلا حبيساً. فاشتركا جميعا فى بنا العس والقيام بامرة وستتى عمسن يعقوب الحبيس لبذا»
It was at this time that this Saint appeared in the region of Arzoun. He performed many miracles. It is said that one day, passing by some people who were cutting vine shoots, he asked them what they were doing. But they, mocking him because of the rags he was wearing, said to him in derision: "We are uprooting vines and planting thorns." He treated them as the prophet Elisha had treated the children who had mocked him: "Let it be," he said to them, "as you say." The vine has remained to this day as an astonishing prodigy; its stems resemble those of the vine, and its leaves those of the thorny boxthorn. His fame having reached the monks, they gathered around him. With the help of a recluse monk, who was found living nearby, he built a convent and they both took care of it. That is why it was called the convent of Jacob the Recluse (Ya'qoub Al-Habis).


[p. 472] {#po.13.p472}
خبر فطاركة العفويّة.
LXIII. - History of the Jacobite patriarchs.
بقى اليعاقبة من ايام يوسطوس والى آخر ايام مورقى بغير فطرك معروف بعد ساورا لاجل قصد ملوك الروم اياهم وطردهم سابورا. وصار لهم اثنان فى السى لم تستقم أحواليم هما. فلما مات مورقى نصبوا رحلا من اهل الرّة يقال له فطرا فطرك عليهم بانطاكية وقلد الاساقفة على المواضع. وكان لهم ايضا بالاسكندرية آخر. فوقع بينهما خلف وحرم كل واحد منهم صاحبه»
After the death of Severus (Säwarra), the Jacobites were deprived of a known patriarch from the reign of Justin (Ioustinos) until the last days of Maurice (Maurikios), because the Greek emperors persecuted them and sought diligently to expel them. However, they had two hidden patriarchs, but they could not settle their affairs. After the death of Maurice, they ordained a patriarch for Antioch (Antakya): he was a man from Callinicum (Raqqa), called Peter (Fatra); he consecrated bishops for several dioceses. They also had another patriarch in Alexandria (Al-Iskandariya). A quarrel arose between these two patriarchs, who were anathematized by one another.

### LXIV. - History of Titus, bishop of Hdatta
خبر ططوس اسقف الحديثة. "كان على قديم الزمان مطران حزةٍ ينظر فى الحديثة ويدبى بيعها. فلما صار فى سئة
From ancient times, Hdatta (Al-Haditha) was under the jurisdiction of the


[p. 473] {#po.13.p473}
ست وستين وتسع مائه للاسكندر الها اصحاب ساونًا ويعقوب واطفوا اهيا وافسدوا ثيانهم واتصل الخر بايشوعيب! الجاثليق فاختار رجلا يقال له ططوس كان اول مجوسيًا فانتضه الله وفتح عين قلبه واعتمد وتعلم فى اسكول المدائن. وجعله اسقف عليها. فطرد البعاقة منها وفضحيم بمناظرته. وأعمد .خلقنً من اهلبا ومن اليهود بها واظهر آيات كثيرة. وابا الاعلاء. واحض رحلا نهشه اففى فمات فغسل صليبه وستقاد الماا. فعاش باذن الله. واجتاز به المومن يزدين لما خرج مع كسرى الى دارا فتبرك منه ودفع اليه ثلثماثة ديثار بى بها بيعة الحدثة. ولما مات ططوس دفن فيها صلاته تحفظتاة
metropolitan of Adiabene (Hazza). The partisans of Severus (Säwarra) and Jacob (Ya'qoub), having gone there in the year 966 of Alexander, seduced the inhabitants and corrupted their minds. The Catholicos Ishoyahb, having learned of this, chose a certain man called Titus (Titous) and consecrated him bishop. The latter had previously professed Magianism; but having been chosen and enlightened by God, he was baptized. He studied in the School of Seleucia. Titus refuted the Jacobites through his controversies and drove them from Hdatta. He baptized many of its inhabitants, and Jews who had settled there. He performed many wonders and healed the sick. By the permission of God, he resurrected a man who had died from a snake bite by making him drink water in which he had dipped his cross. Yazdin, when he accompanied Khosrow during his invasion of the region of Dara, visited him, asked for his blessing, and gave him three hundred dinars for the construction of the church of Hdatta. Titus, after his death, was buried in this church. May his prayers preserve us.


[p. 474] {#po.13.p474}
ذكر خبر القديس. الطاهر من سبريشوع الجاثليق هذا الاب القدّيس من اهل باجرمى من قرية صغيرة تعرف .بفيروزاباد. وكان ابوا يرعى الفنم. فراى فى مامه كان رحِلًا قائمً لابن الوقار واللها يقول له. سيولد لك ابن بات نقتي سمه سبريشوع فان رحا يكون لابشوع! فى كل حاته. ويكون عظيمً طاهرا له منزلة ويعطى رثاسة البيعة ببلاد الفرس. ويصلح الامور البيعّة والمملكة فى رئاسته. ومائر اهل المذاهب يدعونه رئيساً. ويكون لبج بتسابيح الله وحمده. فاخير زوحته بالرويا فحمدت الله وقالت نحن عبيد يفعل نا ما يريد وله نشكر وتحمد. فلما حبلت رات ايضا ” فى منامها انها ولدت ما هى حاملة به وانه ملتحف بازار والجنود تسجد قدامه وكانه جالس على كرسى فى هيكل بيت القدس وعلى راسه كليل من ذهب وجماعة النّاس يزدحمون عليه ويطلبون الدتؤ منه والتبزك به وهو يرسم عليهم رسم الصليب
LXV. - History of the holy and pure Mar Sabrisho' (the thirty-first Catholicos). This holy Father was a native of a small village called Pirozabad, in the land of Beith Garmai (Badjarmi); his father, who was a shepherd, saw in a dream a man standing, clothed in glory and splendor, who said to him: "You will have a just and pious son, and you will call him Sabrisho', for Jesus will be his hope. He will be great and pure; he will rise in dignity and will be the head of the Church of Persia. During his pontificate, he will regulate the affairs of the Church as well as those of the State. The other sects, too, will look upon him as their leader. His only desire will be to glorify God and to thank Him." He told this vision to his wife, who praised God, saying: "We are His servants. May His will be done. We have only to thank Him and praise Him." When she was pregnant, she also saw in a dream that she had brought her son into the world; he was as if seated on a throne in the temple of Jerusalem (Beit el-Qods), covered with a veil and having on his head a crown of gold; soldiers prostrated themselves before him and crowds pressed to approach him


[p. 475] {#po.13.p475}
بين اعينهم وكان قلا يقول لبا هذا هو سبريشوع الذى ات حاملة به. ثم انتيت فسمدت الى الفيرم! فالقت فيه البخور. وحمدت الله على ما اراها. © ثم ولدته بعذ كمال مع الحمل. فلما صار به ابوه الى البيعة ليعمد با" كما يكى الصبيان. فمد ابون يده اليه ليسكته. فرلا احد الافاضل الرهبان كان فى ذلك3 حاضا فى البيعة. فقال له كيف تقدم على ان تم يدك الى رئيس البيعة وفطركها.
and ask for his blessing; and he, he made the sign of the cross on their foreheads. She then heard a voice saying to her: "This is Sabrisho', whom you have conceived." As soon as she awoke, she took the censer; she threw incense into it and thanked God for the vision she had just had. Finally, her time having come, she gave birth to a son; his father carried him to the church to have him baptized. The child having cried as all other children cry, his father raised his hand to silence him. But a virtuous monk, who was then in the church, having seen him (make this gesture): "How dare you," he said to him, "raise your hand against the head and patriarch of the Church?"
ثم بلغ هذا الطاهن الى "سن الحداثة. فاقبيل .يروض تفسه بالمفاوضات الاليّة وقصد نصيبين ايم ابراهم المفتس. فاقام بها لطلب العلم فانجب وبرع. وفطم جسمه من لذّات المطاعم كدانيال النبى. وجعل قوته الحبوب ونات الارض. ثم اقام فى العس زمَانًا طولًا ولم يح الرهبان بتديراته. لانه كان يظهر لهم بالتزام النفقة مهم على الاكل والشرب. وما خفى امرة عليهم. لما تفقدوا تدييرة وما ياخذ به نفيئه تعجُوا منه واجتمعوا اليه يسالونه الصفح عن ذنهم. فيما كانوا بلزمونه من النفقة. بغير واجب" ومن غير ان ياكل مما يتخذ
Arrived at the age of adolescence, this Saint began to practice prayer. He went to Nisibis (Nasibin) to be instructed there by Abraham the Interpreter; he excelled in merit and science. Abstaining, like Daniel, from the pleasures of the table, he fed only on seeds and plants. Then he settled for a long time in the convent where, having committed himself to contribute with the monks to the expense of food, they had no occasion to suspect his way of life. Having finally noticed it, they began to observe him. Amazed at his conduct and his austerities, they came to find him in a chorus to ask his pardon for having unjustly obliged him to make with them the


[p. 476] {#po.13.p476}
من الماكول. فحين علم انهم قد اخوا بفضيلته بعد مقامه تيع سنين تركهم ومضى الى حل يعرف بشعران. فاقام فى كرح! ناا خس منين. واحب الله ان يظهن لقّاس ما دهبه لهذا القتيس. فلبم رجلا كان ممتحاً .يموت اولاد. كان قد مات له ثمنية اولاد بان يصي اليه وساله المصير الى منزله ليعيش" الولد الى يولد له بصلاته ودعائه. فمضى معه ودخل مزله واقبل يِصلّى وضرع بدموع تفيض وطالب بان يزيل الله عن الرجل وزوحته ما امتحنا به من موت اولادهما. وهر طول ليلته. فكان يسمع صوت الشياطين يصيحون على باب الدار افتحوا قد ورد صاحب المنزل. وذلك لان كلمة القنّيس اغلقت فى وجوهبم الابواب والمداخل. واحدق البيت بالصليب. وولدت الامراة ابنا. وفرح به اهله وعشيرّه. واتشر خر القديس فى البلدان العيدة: فابتدا الناس يقصدونه. فهرب .الى القفر واقام فيه منفرماً. فصار "اليه اثنان من الروحانيين مشرانه وقولان ان ربك قد امر ان تمضى
expense of food, of which he took nothing. Seeing then that they had guessed his virtue, he left them after having stayed nine years with them, and went to a mountain, called Sa'ran, where he built a cell and remained there for five years. God, having wished to manifest to men the gifts He had granted to this Saint, inspired a man afflicted by the death of his eight children, whom he had lost one after the other, to go find him to ask him to come to his house, so that through his prayers and requests the child who was to be born to him might live. The Saint went with him; having entered the house, he began to pray to God, shedding tears to ask Him to spare the man and his wife the trial of losing this child as well. He kept vigil all night. The voices of demons were heard, crying at the door of the house: "Open; the master of the house is there." Indeed, the Saint, with a word, had closed the doors and entrances upon them, and had made the sign of the cross all around the house. The woman gave birth to a son, who brought joy to his parents and his family. The fame of the Saint spread to distant regions. To avoid the visits of the crowd, which had begun to come to see him, he withdrew into a desert, to live there in solitude. (One day) two spirits came to him


[p. 477] {#po.13.p477}
وتدبر البعة. وكان معهما كتاب عظيم- فيه كلام. فوضعا على راننه وابتديا بقرآة! على رسم الاسياميذ وترآى له ذلك دفعتين. ودفع اليه عصا الرعاية. وعد ذلك بايام خطبه بالبام روح القدس اهل لاشوم. فعقد له ايشوعيب الجاثليق الاسقفة علييم. ا
and announced to him that his Lord ordered him to go and lead His Church. Then, having placed on his head a large book that they had with them and in which there was writing, they began to read from it the words of the rite of the laying on of hands. They also handed him the pastoral staff. He had this vision twice. A few days later, the inhabitants of Lasom, inspired by the Holy Spirit, came to betroth him (to their church); and the Catholicos Ishoyahb
© وحصل فى الكرسى. فظبرت الآيات والجرائع العجية. فكان منها ان المطر زانى ليلة الشعانين. وعظم وزاد واظلمت الدنيا نبا وكاد العيد يبطل. وحزن الشعب لذلك. قنصب الصلبان واصلح آلات العيد وخرج الى الصحن ورفع يده الى السمآ: فزال البرق والرعذ وسكن المطر. وصحّت التنمآه. وظبرت الشمس وحقت الارض واجتمع الناس وعيّدوا العيد بات فرح بعد اليأن. وقال انه صار بعد ذلك الى الراب فى ايام نيسان وهؤ يطفح بكثرة الم والما يجرى بحدة. فرسم عليه علامة الليب وعبن عليه ولم يترطب قدملا بالمله. وكان هناك رجل
consecrated him their bishop. Once seated on the episcopal throne, he performed amazing wonders and miracles. On the night of Palm Sunday, rain began to fall; it continued to descend in abundance; the day began in darkness; the feast was going to be disrupted; the people were dismayed. Sabrisho' had the crosses raised and the preparations for the feast made: he went out into the courtyard, his hands raised to heaven. The lightning immediately ceased to flash, the thunder to rumble, and the rain to fall; the sun appeared in a clear sky, the earth dried up, and the people, who had despaired, gathered and celebrated the feast with very great joy. It is said that he went after that to the Zab; it was in the month of April; the river had overflowed; the waters were high and very impetuous. The Saint


[p. 478] {#po.13.p478}
من البراطقة المخالفين' ومعه آلة من الفضة والذهب. فال القديس أن بي معه. فصلا وصار تلميناً له وبع ولم يخالفه ولا فارقه8
made the sign of the cross over the waters and crossed the river without even his feet getting wet. A heretic, who was there and who had gold and silver objects on him, begged him to let him cross with him. The Saint let him cross. The dissident became his disciple and accompanied him; he renounced his
وقيل ان رجلا ظالمً حلف بيمين هذا القديس كاذً. فدعا عليه فانشق ومات. وان امراة مجوسية صارت اليه تساله ان يسال ره ان يرزقها ولد تفرح به. فغسل بدا مزّتين يمك ودفعه اليها لتشريه. فاخذته ولم تشريه لتفرنها منه. قبت فى. ذلك المآ عودا الن. فجت وجزعت وشرث المآ فحمات ووضعت ولدين كالغصنين. وامنت واعتمذنت وسائر أهلبا ومن سمع منا خرها»
errors, and never wanted to separate from him. It is also said that the curse of the Saint caused an unjust man, who had falsely sworn by his name, to burst and die. A Magian woman went to find him to ask him to pray to his Lord to grant her a son. He gave her water to drink in which he had just washed his hands twice; the woman took the water, but feeling disgust, she did not drink it; however, astonished and seized with fear to see two sprigs of myrtle growing in it, she consented to drink it. She became pregnant and brought into the world two sons, similar to the two sprigs. She then believed and received baptism with all her relatives and all those who heard her tell her story.
ثم ان النعمن بن المنذر اخا هند ومارية لما احب المسيح ان يهديه مثل اخنيه لانهما كأننا موبنتين قبل اخيهما. وكان النعمن يعبد للاوثان ويذبح الذبائح للزهرة. وكان مع ذلك
Christ wished to convert Al-Nu'man son of Al-Mundhir and brother of Hind and Mary. These had become Christians before their brother. Al-Nu'man worshipped idols and offered sacrifices to Zohra; he was a murderer and bloodthirsty; he could not bear to hear the name of Christ pronounced in his presence nor speak of anything concerning the Christian Church. Mar Simeon (Sem'oun), bishop of Hira, did not cease to pray to Christ to choose Al-Nu'man. God put the latter to the test for three years. When he wanted to sleep after having eaten and drunk,


[p. 479] {#po.13.p479}
قال ستاك للتما لا يحتمل ان يسمع باسم المسيح او بشى من امور البيعة: وكان مار شمعون اسقف الحيرة يديم الصّلاة والمسالة للمنيح ان بجبى النعمن. فمحنه بمحنة ثلث سنين. وهى انه كان إذا اراد الوم بعد الأكل والشرب ترآى له صورتان مختلفتان. احدهما صورة فتى حميل الصورة حلو المنطتق طيب الرائحة واقف بين يديه. والآخر صورة حبشى تبيبح المنظر قائم -خلفه. والفتى الجميل الصورة يفول ان كنت مسيحا فهو خبر لك ليعثم ملكك وبورئك مع الملك الباقى الملك الزائل. فيقول له حاش لى ان اترك سّدتى العرى الالبة1 المدبرةٍ الساكنة فى السم واكون نصراتاً واسجد لرجل صلته البهود. فاذا قال ذاك صرعه الفتى الحشى الذى من ورائه. وكان هذا الحشى كريه الصورة مفزع المنظر منتن الرائحة .يخرج من فيه الرغوة النجسة. فلا يزال من صرعته مغشتاً عليه
